# Flash robustness

Generated by `v -prod -cc gcc run tools/flash_sweep.vsh`: M3 step 9, decision D8, exit criterion 5. Don't edit it by hand; regenerate it after changing the flash.

**What the sweep does.**
- **The points:** 10000 seeded points (seed 20261028), each a PT flash of Peng–Robinson with ChemSep's kᵢⱼ.
- **The compounds:** each point has 2 to 10, drawn from the 640 in the database with Tc, Pc, ω, M and an ideal-gas Cp.
- **The states:** T is log-uniform over 100.0–1000.0 K, P over 0.1–1000.0 bar, and z is uniform over the simplex.
- **The checks,** from scratch on every answer:
  - for a split, exit criterion 3: fugacities to 1e-10, mass balance to 1e-12, Σx = Σy = 1, two distinct phases, and neither unstable;
  - for a single phase, stability re-tested, with five extra random trial phases;
  - for stability, every trial composition's tangent-plane distance recomputed from the model alone, so that the verdict doesn't rest on the flash's own test.
- **The CI subset:** `sweep/sweep_test.v` runs the first 1000 points.

| Outcome | All 10000 | First 1000 (CI) |
|---|---|---|
| One phase, checked stable | 5480 | 564 |
| Two phases, every invariant checked | 3344 | 311 |
| Explained: a third phase (M6) | 1176 | 125 |
| Explained: at a critical point | 0 | 0 |
| **Unexplained** | **0** | **0** |

## The classes

- **A third phase** (`more_phases`). Every two-phase split the flash found, from every start, has a phase that stability proves unstable, by a negative tangent-plane distance (tm, below). There are three or more phases, and M3 handles two: VLLE is M6. The flash refuses rather than return a split it knows is wrong. The sweep proves each such verdict itself: unverified, the flash returns the split it refused, and one of its phases has a trial composition whose tangent-plane distance, computed from the model alone, is below −1e-8. The table gives that phase, the vapour being tried first, and its tm.
- **At a critical point.** The split collapsed onto the feed: every |ln K| < 1e-4, and the two phases can't be told apart.
- **Unexplained:** no convergence, a broken invariant, or a contradiction, such as a single-phase answer that stability calls unstable.
